    uc1:monthsBeforeReceipt 2 .

Checked today, on the hand-written graph (not yet bridge output): usecase1/checks/check_consistency.py recomputes every derived value written in the golden graphs from their dates (PASS golden derived values recompute from the dates (88 values)) and proves the seed rows describe the same applications (PASS seed rows match the golden graphs). Phase 2 gate: the bridge, run on the seed rows, must produce a graph isomorphic graphs to G01.ttl (plus the governing-version triple of 12.4).

12.4 The governing version is resolved done

Reads: the effective dates in usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2025.1.ttl and usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl (the same dates are in uc1_guideline_version); the loan's investor (app:L-1001 uc1:ownedBy ia:InvestorA).
Writes: one triple, app:APP-1001 uc1:governingVersion ia:v2026.1: derived, never stored.

turtle
# investor-a-2025.1.ttl
ia:v2025.1
    a uc1:GuidelineVersion , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    uc1:issuedBy ia:InvestorA ;
    uc1:versionLabel "2025.1" ;
    uc1:effectiveFromDate "2025-01-01T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime ;
    uc1:effectiveToDate "2025-12-31T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime ;
    rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2025.1> .

# investor-a-2026.1.ttl (no effectiveToDate: open-ended)
ia:v2026.1
    a uc1:GuidelineVersion , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    uc1:issuedBy ia:InvestorA ;
    uc1:versionLabel "2026.1" ;
    uc1:effectiveFromDate "2026-01-01T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime ;
    rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2026.1> .

The rule: the investor's version with effectiveFromDate ≤ received date ≤ effectiveToDate, open-ended when there is no end. The received date 2026-02-06 is after 2025-12-31, so v2025.1 is out; it is on or after 2026-01-01, so v2026.1 governs. Exactly one version must match: the SQL rejects two open-ended versions for one investor, and an invariant query catches overlaps. The golden run proves the resolution for every case, including the boundary pair G09 (received 2025-12-31 → v2025.1) and G10 (2026-01-01 → v2026.1):

output (run_golden.py, captured 2026-10-07)
  PASS  G01: governing version by received date is 2026.1

12.5 The data-quality shapes pass done

Reads: usecase1/ontology/uc1-dataquality.shapes.ttl and the graph of 12.3 + 12.4.
Writes: a validation report: conforms true, or one result per data defect.

Before asking "what is missing?", the engine asks "is the data usable?". Three data-quality shapes check it: uc1shape:ApplicationData (exactly one received date-time, exactly one governing version, one hardship reason from the taxonomy, at least one party), uc1shape:PartyData (one role, one employment type, one rental-income flag, one borrower) and uc1shape:DocumentData (one received date-time, classified with exactly one taxonomy concept, confidence within 0..1, an age at receipt that is not negative). They are separate from the template on purpose: a defect here is the servicer's to fix and must never reach a borrower as a "missing document". Planned behaviour (Phase 2): when they do not conform, the engine stops and reports the defect; no notice is drafted.

output (run_golden.py, captured 2026-10-07)
  PASS  G01 @ v2025.1: data-quality shapes conform
  PASS  G01 @ v2026.1: data-quality shapes conform

12.6 The completeness template runs done

Reads: usecase1/ontology/uc1-requirements.shapes.ttl (the template), the governing version's requirements file usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl, the taxonomy (for skos:broader) and the graph.
Writes: the validation report: one result per missing requirement (in production it becomes the items of 12.10).

There is one shape, uc1shape:DocumentRequirement, and it never names a requirement. It is a single SHACL-SPARQL query that reads the requirements as data. For APP-1001 it works like this:

  1. Pick the requirements whose uc1:guidelineVersion is the governing version: R1–R8 of v2026.1.
  2. Drop those that do not apply. Each applicability condition (when a requirement applies: a property (onProperty) that must have a value (expectedValue) on the application or one of its parties) names a property with uc1:onProperty, an annotation property, and the value it must have with uc1:expectedValue; the pair is tested on the application and on every party. R3 needs employmentType Salaried: the party is SelfEmployed, so R3 does not apply. R8 needs hardshipReason Unemployment: it is IncomeReduction, so R8 does not apply. R4 (SelfEmployed) and R7 (hasRentalIncome true) do apply.
  3. Read the recency rule: maxAgeDays, signatureRequired, minCount and mostRecentMonths, each optional (hover a name for its meaning).
  4. Collect the satisfying documents. A document counts when its type is the required concept or a narrower one (the property path uc1:docType/skos:broader*), and it passes every recency filter. Each surviving document adds one key: the document itself, or, when months are counted, its monthsBeforeReceipt, so two statements for the same month count once.
  5. Count. GROUP BY requirement, and HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT key) < minCount (default 1): every requirement that falls short is one violation, whose message is the requirement's own label.

R4 and R5 exactly as they stand in investor-a-2026.1.ttl (the requirement, and its named condition and recency individuals):

turtle
ia26:R4
    a uc1:Requirement , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R4: Profit and loss statement, signed, dated within 90 days of receipt (§4.3.2)"@en ;
    uc1:requirementId "R4" ;
    uc1:guidelineVersion ia:v2026.1 ;
    uc1:documentType uc1tx:ProfitAndLoss ;
    uc1:condition ia26:R4-condition ;
    uc1:recency ia26:R4-recency ;
    uc1:guideSection ia26:s4.3.2 .

ia26:R4-condition
    a uc1:ApplicabilityCondition , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R4 applies when employmentType = SelfEmployed"@en ;
    uc1:onProperty uc1:employmentType ;
    uc1:expectedValue uc1tx:SelfEmployed .

ia26:R4-recency
    a uc1:RecencyRule , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R4 recency: maxAgeDays 90, signatureRequired true"@en ;
    uc1:maxAgeDays 90 ;
    uc1:signatureRequired true .

ia26:R5
    a uc1:Requirement , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R5: Two most recent monthly bank statements for every account (§4.4)"@en ;
    uc1:requirementId "R5" ;
    uc1:guidelineVersion ia:v2026.1 ;
    uc1:documentType uc1tx:BankStatement ;
    uc1:recency ia26:R5-recency ;
    uc1:guideSection ia26:s4.4 .

ia26:R5-recency
    a uc1:RecencyRule , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R5 recency: minCount 2, mostRecentMonths 2"@en ;
    uc1:minCount 2 ;
    uc1:mostRecentMonths 2 .

Why R4 fails. The only candidate is D3 (ProfitAndLoss). Its age is 98 days, more than maxAgeDays 90, so the age filter removes it; it is also unsigned (uc1:signed false) while signatureRequired true, so the signature filter would remove it too. Zero keys < 1: R4 is missing. To satisfy it the borrower must send a signed P&L dated on or after 2025-11-08 (2026-02-06 − 90 days).

Why R5 fails. The only candidate is D4 (BankStatement). There is no age limit; its monthsBeforeReceipt is 2, inside the window 1…2, so it stays and contributes the key "2" (December). One distinct month < minCount 2: R5 is missing. The month that would complete it is month 1, January 2026, for every account.

RequirementApplies?Satisfying documents (v2026.1)Outcome
R1 MAA, signedyes (no condition)D1 (signed)met
R2 Hardship statementyesD2met
R3 Two paystubs ≤ 90 daysno: not Salaried—not applicable
R4 P&L, signed, ≤ 90 daysyes: SelfEmployednone (D3: 98 days, unsigned)missing
R5 Two most recent monthly bank statementsyesD4 covers December only (1 of 2 months)missing
R6 Form 4506-C, signedyesD5 (signed)met
R7 Leaseyes: hasRentalIncome trueD6 (no recency rule, so 250 days is fine)met
R8 Benefit award letterno: hardship is not Unemployment—not applicable

# Prints the full pySHACL report for G01 under every guide version (run_golden.py --show-report prints a

@'
import sys

import common, run_golden
from rdflib import Graph, URIRef
shapes = Graph().parse(common.SHAPES_TTL, format="turtle")
case = Graph().parse(common.GOLDEN_DIR / "G01.ttl", format="turtle")
app = run_golden.APP["APP-1001"]
for version in sorted(common.VERSION_FILES):
    data = run_golden._base(version) + case
    data.add((app, run_golden.UC1.governingVersion, URIRef(common.VERSION_IRIS[version])))
    ids, text = run_golden.missing_ids(data, shapes)
    print("v" + version, ids)
    print(text)

12.7 The due date planned

Reads: uc1_application.received_at and uc1_holiday (22 rows for 2025–2026 in usecase1/sql/uc1_seed.sql, seeded today). The clock code that computes the date is planned (Phase 2); the dates below were verified by hand against the seeded holidays.
Writes: the due date of the acknowledgement notice (the Reg X written notice due within 5 business days of receipt: it says the application is complete, or lists the documents still missing), frozen later in uc1_evaluation.due_date (12.10). Only an incomplete application has one.

Rule (12 CFR 1024.41(b)(2)(i)(B), the acknowledgement notice): the 5th business day after the date part of received_at, skipping Saturdays, Sundays and every legal public holiday (a US federal holiday as observed; it is not a business day for the Reg X clock and is kept in uc1_holiday) in uc1_holiday. Worked out by hand with the seed's holiday rows:

ReceivedDay 1Day 2Day 3Day 4Day 5 = dueSkipped
Fri 2026-02-06 (APP-1001)Mon 02-09Tue 02-10Wed 02-11Thu 02-12Fri 2026-02-13Sat 02-07, Sun 02-08. Washington's Birthday (Mon 02-16) is after the window.
Thu 2026-02-12 (clock test)Fri 02-13Tue 02-17Wed 02-18Thu 02-19Fri 2026-02-20Sat 02-14, Sun 02-15, Mon 02-16 Washington's Birthday

The holiday row this depends on, as seeded: ('2026-02-16', 'Washington''s Birthday'). Both dates above were checked by hand, day by day, against that table; they are the expected values for the Phase 2 clock tests (weekend, holiday inside the window, receipt late on a Friday), which will be the first time code computes them.

12.8 Citations planned

Reads: the guide sections the failed requirements point at (uc1:guideSection in the version file), and the synthetic guide for v2026.1, usecase1/guides/investor-a-servicing-guide-v2026.1.md (planned), ingested into RAG-OS (Phase 4).
Writes: one section-level citation per missing item (Markdown has no pages, so the citation points at a heading, not a page), shown to the reviewer and quoted in the letter.

turtle
# investor-a-2026.1.ttl: the sections R4 and R5 cite
ia26:s4.3.2
    a uc1:GuideSection , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "§4.3.2 Self-employed income"@en ;
    uc1:sectionNumber "4.3.2" ;
    rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2026.1#section-4.3.2> .

ia26:s4.4
    a uc1:GuideSection , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "§4.4 Asset verification"@en ;
    uc1:sectionNumber "4.4" ;
    rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2026.1#section-4.4> .

How it will work (Phase 4, section 9). Each guide version is a synthetic Markdown file, fixed in step 4.2: usecase1/guides/investor-a-servicing-guide-v2025.1.md and usecase1/guides/investor-a-servicing-guide-v2026.1.md. Its front matter (a YAML block at the top of a Markdown file (between --- lines); RAG-OS reads effective_date from it) carries effective_date (RAG-OS reads that field only from .md/.txt front matter), and it has one ## heading per guide section, written as <sectionNumber> <label>, e.g. 4.3.2 Self-employed income and 4.4 Asset verification. A RAG-OS citation carries doc_id, chunk_id, title, path, page, heading, score, snippet; for Markdown page stays empty and heading is the heading path, the headings above the chunk joined with " > ", e.g. Investor A Servicing Guide v2026.1 (SYNTHETIC) > 4.3.2 Self-employed income. The resolver searches only the governing version's guide and accepts a hit only when the last segment of its heading equals "<sectionNumber> <label>" exactly ("4.3.2 Self-employed income"); several matching places, or none, leave the item "citation unresolved", never guessed. Where the citation is stored is a Phase 4 decision.

12.9 The letter draft and the verifier planned

Reads: the engine's missing items (R4, R5 with their labels), their citations (12.8) and the due date (12.7).
Writes: a draft letter for the reviewer, and the verifier's pass/fail.

The draft is written through RAG-OS's LLM port (token use recorded). The LLM only words the letter; it never decides what is missing. The verifier then checks, without any LLM, that the draft lists exactly R4 and R5: nothing added, nothing dropped, no item reworded beyond the allowed template. For APP-1001 the content must amount to: a signed profit and loss statement dated on or after 2025-11-08 (§4.3.2), and the January 2026 bank statement for every account (§4.4). A draft that fails the verifier is never shown as ready (Phase 5, section 10).

12.10 Human approval; the evaluation is frozen planned

Reads: the reviewer's approval in the Admin workbench (Phase 3).
Writes: the evaluation log (the append-only rows in uc1_evaluation, uc1_evaluation_item and uc1_evaluation_item_document that freeze one decision): uc1_evaluation, uc1_evaluation_item and uc1_evaluation_item_document (tables exist; the engine that writes them is Phase 2). Today they hold 0 rows.

A person approves the result and the letter. The engine then writes one evaluation, one item per requirement of the governing version and one link per satisfying document. It is append-only: a re-evaluation adds a new evaluation, so a later edit to a rule or a row never changes a past decision, and the evaluation records the version it used (PROV-O prov:used). Illustrative rows for APP-1001, following the id and reason rules of step 2.6 (section 7): evaluation_id = ev_ + a uuid4 hex; evaluation_item_id = <evaluation_id>/R1 … /R8; evaluated_at in UTC; reason = NULL for a met item (its linked documents are the evidence) and the requirement's rdfs:label, verbatim, for a missing or not_applicable item, never a name or a borrower id. The uuid, engine version and time are placeholders:

sql

D4 is not linked to R5: only a met item has satisfying documents, an invariant check_sql.py already tests. Two more invariants apply: a linked document belongs to the evaluated application, and was received by evaluated_at (hence 15:35 after a 15:30 receipt).

12.11 The same package under v2025.1 gives only R5 done

The golden run also evaluates every case under the version that does not govern, to prove that the version matters. Under v2025.1 APP-1001 misses only R5, because R4 was different then:

turtle
# investor-a-2025.1.ttl
ia25:R4-recency
    a uc1:RecencyRule , owl:NamedIndividual ;
    rdfs:label "R4 recency: maxAgeDays 120"@en ;
    uc1:maxAgeDays 120 .
  • R4 is met: the limit was 120 days and there was no signature rule. D3 is 98 days old (≤ 120), and being unsigned does not matter.
  • R5 is still missing: R5 is identical in both versions, and one December statement is still one month of the two.
  • R8 does not exist in v2025.1 (it was added in v2026.1); it would not apply to APP-1001 anyway.

Constraint Violation in SPARQLConstraintComponent (http://www.w3.org/ns/shacl#SPARQLConstraintComponent):
    Severity: sh:Violation
    Source Shape: uc1shape:DocumentRequirement
    Focus Node: app:APP-1001
    Value Node: app:APP-1001
    Source Constraint: [ … trimmed … ]
    Message: R5: Two most recent monthly bank statements for every account (§4.4)

This is why the governing version is resolved explicitly (12.4) and recorded with every evaluation: the same six documents give two different notices depending on one date.

13Change recipes#

The five changes a team makes most often, written for someone doing them the first time. Each one follows the loop of section 4: change the file in its tool, run the checks, review the git diff, record the change. Two habits make every recipe safe: work test-first (change the expected answer before the rule, watch the check fail, then make it pass; proves the check sees the change) (change the expected answers before the rule), and change the README naming contract first, then the contract maps (the copy of the README naming contract hard-coded in a check script (CONTRACT, HIERARCHY, EXPECTED_…); changed in the same pull request as the README) in the check scripts, in the same pull request.

13.1Add a new guide version (e.g. v2027.1)
When
Investor A publishes a new servicing guide. A new guide is never an edit of the old file: the old version still governs every application received while it was in effect.
Who
Knowledge engineer (KE: files and Protégé); SME (the requirement content, from the new guide); compliance (the golden expectations); an engineer (the check scripts); a reviewer.
Tools and files
  • PowerShell / VS Code: new usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl; usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl (close it); usecase1/ontology/catalog-v001.xml
  • The requirement changes in usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl: in VS Code with Protégé read-only, or in Protégé, by the formatting policy of step 1.2r (step 10 below)
  • VS Code: usecase1/checks/common.py, usecase1/checks/check_ontology.py, usecase1/golden/expected.yaml, usecase1/sql/uc1_seed.sql, usecase1/README.md
  • Exists today: the 2025.1 and 2026.1 files only. Everything named 2027.1 is created by this recipe.
Steps
  1. Copy the current version:
    powershell
    Copy-Item usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl
  2. In VS Code, in the new file only, use plain find-and-replace (not regex): 2026.1 → 2027.1, then ia26 → ia27. That changes, in one reviewable edit: the ontology IRI <https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-a/2027.1>, the owl:versionInfo "2027.1", the dct:title, the version individual ia:v2027.1 with uc1:versionLabel "2027.1" and rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2027.1>, every uc1:guidelineVersion, the section links, and the prefix line, which becomes @prefix ia27: <https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-a/2027.1#> . Note: unlike the core (which has one), the version files have no owl:versionIRI (an owl:versionIRI naming one specific version of an ontology, so tools can tell versions apart); the version lives in the ontology IRI itself and in owl:versionInfo (the catalog check accepts either). Text editing is used here because renaming IRIs in Protégé is entity by entity.
  3. Still in the new file, set uc1:effectiveFromDate "2027-01-01T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime on ia:v2027.1, give it no effectiveToDate (open-ended), and update the header comment.
  4. Close the old version: in investor-a-2026.1.ttl add uc1:effectiveToDate "2026-12-31T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime ; to ia:v2026.1 (inclusive last day, written as a date-time at midnight). This is the only edit to a published version file.
  5. Add the catalog entry, next to the others in catalog-v001.xml:
    xml
    <uri id="investor-a-2027.1" name="https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-a/2027.1" uri="investor-a-2027.1.ttl"/>
  6. In usecase1/checks/common.py: add "2027.1": ONTOLOGY_DIR / "investor-a-2027.1.ttl" to VERSION_FILES, "ia27": "https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-a/2027.1#" to NS, and "2027.1": NS["ia"] + "v2027.1" to VERSION_IRIS. From now on every check loads the new file; run_golden.py needs no change (it prints one column per version).
  7. Test first, expected answers: in usecase1/golden/expected.yaml add the new version's expected ids (a "2027.1": [...] list under missing:) for every case; run_golden FAILs any case without them (FAIL G01 @ v2027.1: has an expectation in expected.yaml). Compliance writes the lists from the new guide, by hand, never from a run. governs stays 2026.1 for G01–G08, G10, G11 (2025.1 for G09). Add a boundary pair received 2026-12-31 / 2027-01-01 (13.4).
  8. Test first, expected diff: in check_ontology.py add "2027.1" to EXPECTED_REQUIREMENT_IDS, and add the expected diff for 2026.1 → 2027.1 in check_ontology._check_version_diff (which requirements the new guide changes, adds, removes). Today that function asserts only 2025.1 → 2026.1 (when both files are present) and, for any other version, prints info no expected diff is defined for ['2027.1']: add one to _check_version_diff (recipe 13.1). That is an info line, not a FAIL, so this step is easy to forget: the gate review looks for it.
  9. Run the golden and ontology checks: the new file is still a copy of 2026.1, so every case whose answer the new guide changes FAILs, and so does the new diff. That proves the checks can see the change.
  10. Make the requirement changes in investor-a-2027.1.ttl (add or remove requirements as in 13.3), by the formatting policy recorded in step 1.2r:
    • The team keeps hand-formatted files (Protégé read-only): edit investor-a-2027.1.ttl in VS Code, e.g. in ia27:R4-recency change uc1:maxAgeDays 90 ;; copy an existing requirement block (requirement, condition, recency, section) to add one. Then open the file in Protégé only to reason: File → Open → investor-a-2027.1.ttl, Reasoner → HermiT, Reasoner → Start reasoner (no red, no inconsistency dialog). Close Protégé without saving.
    • The team accepts Protégé's formatting: edit in Protégé. File → Open → investor-a-2027.1.ttl; View → Render by label; Entities → Individuals → e.g. ia27:R4-recency → Property assertions → change the value; Reasoner → HermiT, then Reasoner → Start reasoner (no inconsistency); File → Save. The save rewrites the whole file (comments go, order changes).
    • Either way: git diff -- usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl (look for the semantic change only), then run_all.py.
  11. In uc1_seed.sql: set '2026-12-31' as effective_to_date of investor-a/2026.1, add ('investor-a/2027.1', 'investor-a', '2027.1', '2027-01-01', NULL, 'ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl', NULL), and add the 2027 federal holidays to the uc1_holiday insert (today it holds 2025–2026 only, 22 rows; the clock needs the new year). The schema itself guards you: a second open-ended version for one investor is rejected, and the overlap invariant catches overlapping dates.
  12. In usecase1/README.md: add the ia27: row to the Prefixes table.
  13. Run the checks below until everything passes.
  14. Phase 4 (planned): write and ingest usecase1/guides/investor-a-servicing-guide-v2027.1.md (as step 4.2: front matter effective_date: 2027-01-01, one heading per section) and fill source_doc_id of its version row.
⚙ Checks
powershell
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with owlready2 python usecase1/checks/check_ontology.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_golden.py
uv run --no-project python usecase1/checks/check_sql.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 python usecase1/checks/check_consistency.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with owlready2 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_all.py

Expect the ontology section to list investor-a-2027.1.ttl with the same five lines as the other version files (parses, no uc1 declarations, requirement ids, issuer and date, every requirement complete), a PASS line for each expected diff (2025.1 → 2026.1 and 2026.1 → 2027.1) and no no expected diff is defined info line, three version columns in the golden table, and == ALL PASS.

🔍 Review
  • git diff usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl shows only the new effectiveToDate line.
  • git diff --no-index usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2026.1.ttl usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl shows the renamed IRIs plus exactly the requirement changes the guide makes; the SME ticks each against the guide text. (If the team accepts Protégé's formatting, the saved file is reordered and this text diff is noisy: rely on the version-diff check of the next item instead.)
  • The expected 2026.1 → 2027.1 diff in _check_version_diff matches the guide's change list, and check_ontology prints its PASS line.
  • Compliance signs the new expected.yaml lists before the rule edits are merged.
Ledger and docs
  • ledger (CHANGES.md): the existing row "usecase1/ontology/*.ttl … one requirements file per guide version" already covers the file; name the version in the pull request. Phase 4 adds the guide document under the existing "Synthetic guide documents" row.
  • README: the new prefix. Design (UseCase1.html section 7): update any text that lists the versions.
13.2Add a new document type (e.g. W2)
When
A requirement needs a document the taxonomy cannot name yet. Do this only after step 1.1u, when the VocBench 3 project exists and its export is the source of truth.
Who
SME (proposes the concept in VocBench), a second SME as validator (approves it in the validation workflow), an engineer (contract maps), KE (if a requirement will use it: 13.3).
Tools and files
  • VS Code: usecase1/README.md (taxonomy table), usecase1/checks/check_taxonomy.py (CONTRACT, HIERARCHY)
  • VocBench project uc1-loss-mitigation → export to usecase1/taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl
  • Phase 2 (planned): the converter regenerates usecase1/taxonomy/taxonomy.yaml
Steps
  1. README first: in the uc1_doc_type row, under Income, add **W2**: Form W-2; *W-2, wage and tax statement*.
  2. In check_taxonomy.py: add "W2": ("Form W-2", frozenset({"W-2", "wage and tax statement"})) to CONTRACT["uc1_doc_type"] and "W2": "Income" to HIERARCHY. EXPECTED derives from CONTRACT; TOP_CONCEPTS does not change (W2 is not a top concept). On the export the check asserts ids, hierarchy and top concepts; labels are compared with the contract only on the seed.
  3. In VocBench: open the project; Data menu → Concept tab; select Income and use the tab's create-narrower action. Give a specified IRI (a VocBench concept created with an IRI you type (local name = notation) instead of a generated random c_… IRI): https://rag-os.example/uc1/taxonomy#W2 (local name = notation = the future facet value id). Add prefLabel "Form W-2"@en, altLabels "W-2"@en and "wage and tax statement"@en, skos:notation "W2" and a definition@en (every concept needs one: the classifier reads it). The change stays pending until the validator approves it.
  4. Export: Global Data Management → Export Data → format Turtle → save over usecase1/taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl.
  5. Close the loop: compare the new export with the previous one, so the diff shows only W2 (VocBench may reorder the file; --compare compares content, not text). The one-time --roundtrip gate compared the seed with the first export and is not repeated: after real edits the two are meant to differ.
  6. Nothing else changes: the core has no document-type classes (types are data), classification_doc_type is TEXT and will hold 'W2', the template is untouched. W2 only affects completeness once a requirement names uc1tx:W2 (13.3).
  7. Phase 2 (planned): the converter regenerates taxonomy.yaml; bump the facets version (the version: number at the top of a RAG-OS facets file; bump it so the classifier refreshes its cached prototypes) so the classifier refreshes. Phase 5: add labelled W-2 samples to the classifier accuracy set.
⚙ Checks
powershell
git show HEAD:usecase1/taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl > $env:TEMP\uc1-prev.skos.ttl
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 python usecase1/checks/check_taxonomy.py --compare $env:TEMP\uc1-prev.skos.ttl usecase1/taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 python usecase1/checks/check_taxonomy.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with owlready2 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_all.py
🔍 Review
  • VocBench: the validator approved the proposal (History shows it).
  • The compare output lists exactly one added concept, W2, under Income.
  • README table, CONTRACT and HIERARCHY changed in the same pull request.
Ledger and docs
  • No new ledger row: the export row covers the file. Mention the new concept in the pull request.
If VocBench generated an IRI like …#c_5f3a…, delete the concept and create it again with the specified IRI; check_taxonomy.py fails "notation == IRI local name" otherwise.
13.3Add or change a requirement
When
A new guide version adds or changes a rule (inside 13.1), or a transcription error in an existing version must be fixed. A published version is never edited to follow a newer guide.
Who
SME (the rule, from the guide text), compliance (the expected answers), KE (the version file), an engineer (check expectations), a reviewer.
Tools and files
  • VS Code first: usecase1/golden/expected.yaml, a golden case that exercises the rule (usecase1/golden/G12.ttl, see 13.4), usecase1/checks/check_ontology.py (EXPECTED_REQUIREMENT_IDS, the expected diff in _check_version_diff)
  • The version file, e.g. usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl: in VS Code with Protégé read-only, or in Protégé, by the formatting policy of step 1.2r
Steps
  1. Test first: compliance updates the affected version's lists in expected.yaml and, if no case exercises the rule, adds one (13.4), e.g. a P&L 95 days old when the limit moves. The engineer updates EXPECTED_REQUIREMENT_IDS (a new id) and the expected diff in _check_version_diff (changed / added).
  2. Run the golden cases: the affected lines must now FAIL, e.g. FAIL G12 @ v2027.1: missing none: expected ['R9']. This proves the cases can see the change.
  3. Decide the content (the same in either tool). To change a rule: edit a value on its individual, e.g. ia27:R4-recency. To add R9, write these named individuals (never blank nodes, so each stays editable and diffable): ia27:R9 (type Requirement), ia27:R9-condition (ApplicabilityCondition, with uc1:onProperty and uc1:expectedValue as annotations), ia27:R9-recency (RecencyRule, with its values as property assertions), and the section ia27:s4.x (GuideSection, uc1:sectionNumber, rdfs:seeAlso <guide://investor-a/2027.1#section-4.x>). On ia27:R9 set: rdfs:label "R9: … (§4.x)"@en (the label must contain the section number), uc1:requirementId "R9" (must match the end of the IRI), uc1:guidelineVersion ia:v2027.1, uc1:documentType, uc1:condition, uc1:recency, uc1:guideSection.
  4. onProperty must be a uc1 property declared in the core, and expectedValue a taxonomy concept or a typed literal ("true"^^xsd:boolean). A rule that needs a new kind of recency parameter is not a data change: it changes the core and the template (an engineer, design review).
  5. Make the edit by the formatting policy recorded in step 1.2r:
    • The team keeps hand-formatted files (Protégé read-only): edit investor-a-2027.1.ttl in VS Code, e.g. in ia27:R4-recency change uc1:maxAgeDays 90 ;; copy an existing requirement block (requirement, condition, recency, section) to add one. Then open the file in Protégé only to reason: File → Open → investor-a-2027.1.ttl, Reasoner → HermiT, Reasoner → Start reasoner (no red, no inconsistency dialog). Close Protégé without saving.
    • The team accepts Protégé's formatting: edit in Protégé. File → Open → investor-a-2027.1.ttl; View → Render by label; Entities → Individuals → e.g. ia27:R4-recency → Property assertions → change the value; Reasoner → HermiT, then Reasoner → Start reasoner (no inconsistency); File → Save. The save rewrites the whole file (comments go, order changes).
    • Either way: git diff -- usecase1/ontology/investor-a-2027.1.ttl (look for the semantic change only), then run_all.py.
  6. Run the checks below until they pass.
⚙ Checks
powershell
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_golden.py --case G12 --show-report
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with owlready2 python usecase1/checks/check_ontology.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with owlready2 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_all.py
🔍 Review
  • The SME reads the new label next to the guide sentence.
  • The KE confirms the condition property and value.
  • The reviewer confirms the expectation changed before the rule (commit order, or the failing run pasted in the pull request).
Ledger and docs
  • No ledger row (requirements are data). If the core or the template changes, record it in the pull request and update UseCase1.html section 7.
13.4Add a new golden case
When
A scenario is not covered yet, a rule changes (13.3), or a new version needs a boundary pair (13.1).
Who
Compliance (the expected answer, by hand), an engineer or KE (the Turtle and the seed rows), a second reader (recomputes the derived values).
Tools and files
  • VS Code: new usecase1/golden/G12.ttl, usecase1/golden/expected.yaml, usecase1/sql/uc1_seed.sql
Steps
  1. Take the next id and keep the naming pattern: case G12 ↔ application APP-1012, loan L-1012, borrower B1012, party APP-1012-P1, documents APP-1012-D1…
  2. Copy G01.ttl to G12.ttl; rewrite the header comment (one-line scenario, SYNTHETIC), then the application, loan, parties and documents. Dates are xsd:dateTime at midnight; receivedAt keeps its time. Recompute ageAtReceiptDays (only when there is a document date) and monthsBeforeReceipt (only when there is a period end) with the README rules. Never write uc1:governingVersion (the runner adds it per version) and never a name.
  3. Add the case to expected.yaml: application, title, received_at, governs, and a missing list for every version in VERSION_FILES, written by compliance from the requirements, never by running the engine.
  4. Add the same facts to uc1_seed.sql, copying the G01 block: loan, borrower, application, parties, documents, with identical dates, flags and types.
  5. Run the checks; fix whichever side is wrong.
⚙ Checks
powershell
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 python usecase1/checks/check_consistency.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_golden.py --case G12 --show-report
uv run --no-project python usecase1/checks/check_sql.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with owlready2 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_all.py

check_consistency.py proves the derived values recompute and that the seed rows match the graph; run_golden.py proves the expected answer; check_sql.py proves the seed still loads and the invariants hold.

🔍 Review
  • Compliance signs the expectation; the second reader recomputes every derived value by hand.
  • The case tests one thing, and its title says what.
Ledger and docs
  • No new row (golden/ is covered), but the ledger says "11 golden application graphs": update the count. Update the count in UseCase1.html sections 10 and 12 as well.
13.5Add a second investor
When
Loans owned by another investor, with its own guide, come into scope.
Who
KE, SME for the new guide, compliance, an engineer (checks and, later, the bridge).
Tools and files
  • New usecase1/ontology/investor-b-2026.1.ttl (one per version), usecase1/ontology/catalog-v001.xml, usecase1/README.md, usecase1/sql/uc1_seed.sql, usecase1/golden/ + expected.yaml, usecase1/checks/common.py, check_ontology.py, run_golden.py
Steps
  1. README first: prefixes ib: = https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-b# and, per version, ib26: = https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-b/2026.1#.
  2. Create each version file as in 13.1, with ib:InvestorB (a uc1:Investor), ib:v2026.1 uc1:issuedBy ib:InvestorB, its own sections and requirements, ontology IRI https://rag-os.example/uc1/investor-b/2026.1; add a catalog entry per file.
  3. Re-key the checks by investor: today common.VERSION_FILES and VERSION_IRIS are keyed by the version label alone ("2026.1" would collide) and check_ontology.version_ontology_iri() hard-codes investor-a. Key them by guideline_version_id (investor-b/2026.1), and key missing: in expected.yaml the same way.
  4. Resolve the governing version per investor: follow the loan (uc1:loan → uc1:ownedBy) and choose only among that investor's versions. Today run_golden._effective_ranges() looks at every version file; restrict it to the case's investor, and evaluate each case only under its own investor's versions.
  5. Seed: an uc1_investor row ('investor-b', 'Investor B (synthetic)', 'IB'), its uc1_guideline_version rows, and loans with investor_id = 'investor-b'. The constraints are already per investor (unique label, one open-ended version, no overlap).
  6. Golden cases whose loan is uc1:ownedBy ib:InvestorB (13.4), including a pair where both investors receive the same package on the same day and get different answers.
  7. Phase 4 (planned): the investor's guide documents; Phase 3: the workbench lists versions per investor.
⚙ Checks
powershell
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with owlready2 python usecase1/checks/check_ontology.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_golden.py
uv run --no-project python usecase1/checks/check_sql.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 python usecase1/checks/check_consistency.py
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with owlready2 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_all.py
🔍 Review
  • Does not change: uc1-core.ttl (Investor, issuedBy and ownedBy already model it), the template (it only follows governingVersion), the data-quality shapes, the DDL (investor_id is already a key everywhere), the clock (Reg X does not depend on the investor) and the taxonomy, unless the new guide needs new document types (13.2).
  • Does change: prefixes, the investor individual, version files, catalog, seed rows, the checks' keys, golden cases and the per-investor resolution of the governing version.
Ledger and docs
  • New files sit in usecase1/ (covered). Phase 4 guide documents: extend the "Synthetic guide documents" row with the investor; extend removal step 1 (filter by investor).

14Quality gates and test matrix#

A phase closes only when its gate holds. Every criterion below is something a person can check: a command prints PASS, a report exists, a named person signed. As in the RACI of section 3, the product owner is accountable for every gate; the last column names who must sign off before the product owner closes it.

14.1 Phase gates

15Troubleshooting and FAQ#

15.1 Symptom, cause, fix

SymptomCauseFix
VocBench 3: NoSuchSettingsManager error in the console right after startThe browser sent a request before Semantic Turkey finished starting.Wait for Started Application in … seconds, then refresh the page. Harmless.
VocBench console: a warning about JwtDecoder and example-issuer-uriA placeholder OAuth setting in the default configuration; OAuth is not used locally.Ignore it.
http://localhost:1979/vocbench3 does not loadThe console window running semanticturkey.bat was closed (that stops the server), or another program holds port 1979.Start C:\Tools\vocbench3-15.1.2-full\bin\semanticturkey.bat again and leave the window open. Find a port holder with Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 1979 | Select-Object OwningProcess, then Get-Process -Id <pid>.
uv fails with a certificate error (e.g. invalid peer certificate: UnknownIssuer)A TLS-inspecting proxy or antivirus (AVG on the team's machines) re-signs HTTPS traffic.Add --native-tls right after uv run, or set $env:UV_NATIVE_TLS = "1" for the session.
FAIL HermiT ran: … in check_ontology (Java not found, or the wrong Java)owlready2 needs java; JAVA_HOME is unset, or points at a removed JDK.Look at the info java: … line. Set $env:JAVA_HOME = "C:\Program Files\Eclipse Adoptium\jdk-21.0.11.10-hotspot" (the path printed on 2026-10-07) and check java -version. run_all.py --no-reasoner skips HermiT for a quick run, never for a gate.
Protégé says an imported ontology could not be loadedThe catalog-v001.xml entry is missing or wrong: name must equal the imported ontology IRI exactly, and uri is a path relative to the catalog's folder (usecase1/ontology/), e.g. ../taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl.Fix catalog-v001.xml and reopen the file. check_ontology reports the catalog's entries, missing targets and declared IRIs.
HermiT (Protégé or check_ontology): UnsupportedDatatypeExceptionAn xsd:date literal. OWL 2 has no xsd:date, and HermiT refuses datatypes outside the OWL 2 datatype map.Write the date at midnight: "2027-01-01T00:00:00"^^xsd:dateTime. The check "only OWL 2 datatypes in the import closure" lists the offending datatypes.
check_taxonomy.py --roundtrip fails after the exportVocBench changed IRIs (generated c_… local names because no IRI was specified) or labels / language tags, or edits were made after the load.Fix it in VocBench (specified IRI = notation) and export again. If a difference is intended, change the README contract and the contract maps in the same pull request.
check_ontology: FAIL catalog targets are current (the taxonomy entry points at uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl): drift: https://rag-os.example/uc1/taxonomy -> uc1-taxonomy.seed.skos.ttl, expected uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttlcatalog drift (catalog-v001.xml points at another file than the checks expect, e.g. still the seed after the VocBench export exists): the export exists, but the catalog still points at the seed (or a version file was renamed).Set the taxonomy entry's uri to ../taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl (the catalog switch of step 1.1u).
run_golden: FAIL G01 @ v2026.1: missing ['R5']: expected ['R4', 'R5']The rule, the case graph or the expectation changed.Read the report: 15.2.
run_golden: FAIL G01 @ v2027.1: has an expectation in expected.yaml (and "no expectation" in that case's column of the summary table)A version was added to VERSION_FILES, but expected.yaml has no missing list for it in this case.Add the new version's expected ids for every case in expected.yaml, written by compliance (recipe 13.1, step 7).
check_ontology: info no expected diff is defined for ['2027.1']: add one to _check_version_diff (recipe 13.1)A version file exists that no expected diff covers. Only 2025.1 → 2026.1 is asserted; this is an info line, not a FAIL, so ALL PASS can still print.Add the expected diff for 2026.1 → 2027.1 in check_ontology._check_version_diff (recipe 13.1, step 8); the gate review checks that this line is gone.
Running uc1_create.sql again fails with table uc1_investor already existsThe create script expects an empty schema and has no IF NOT EXISTS, on purpose: a half-built schema should fail loudly. (check_sql.py deletes .data/uc1.db first, and its line "fails loudly on an existing schema" is a PASS by design.)Run uc1_drop.sql first (it is idempotent), then the create script. On PostgreSQL you can instead run DROP SCHEMA uc1 CASCADE; and re-create the schema.
check_sql: PermissionError on .data\uc1.dbAnother program (a database browser, a Python shell) has the file open, so it cannot be deleted.Close that program and run again.
docker is not recognised, docker compose (Docker's command to start the services of a compose file; built into Docker Desktop, written as two words) is unknown, or error during connectDocker Desktop is not running (step 0.5 is open), or an old install only has docker-compose.Start Docker Desktop and wait until the engine runs; docker version must show a Server section and docker compose version must answer.
Garbled characters (Â§, â†’) or UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec can't encode characterThe Windows console uses cp1252, not UTF-8 (the text encoding of every file here; the Windows console defaults to cp1252, which cannot print §, → or ✓). The checks reconfigure their output in common.py; your own scripts do not (this exact error occurred while this runbook was written).$env:PYTHONIOENCODING = "utf-8" for your scripts; [Console]::OutputEncoding = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8 for the console.
Protégé reformatted my file (comments gone, order changed)Expected: Protégé writes the whole file again on save.Follow the formatting policy recorded in step 1.2r. Hand-formatted files: do not keep the save; git restore the file and make the edit in VS Code (Protégé read-only). Protégé's formatting accepted: review with git diff for the semantic change, then run the checks. Either way, keep explanations in the README, not in comments a save will drop.

SQLite and PostgreSQL differ in ways that matter here (the scripts are written to run on both; step 1.7b proves it):

TopicSQLitePostgreSQL
BooleansNo boolean type: TRUE/FALSE are stored as 1/0 (see the output in 12.2); a CHECK keeps out text such as 'yes'.A real BOOLEAN; prints t/f; rejects 'yes' by type.
Floating pointDOUBLE PRECISION gets REAL affinity (8-byte float).DOUBLE PRECISION. On both, the bridge converts to xsd:decimal, which the data-quality shape expects.
DatesNo date type: DATE columns hold text 'YYYY-MM-DD'; comparisons are string comparisons, correct only because the format is ISO (check_sql validates every value).Parses and rejects invalid dates; returns date objects.
TimestampsText 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS'.TIMESTAMP without time zone; the values are UTC by convention (_at columns).
NamespaceOne file, .data/uc1.db.Schema uc1: CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS uc1; SET search_path TO uc1; before the scripts; removal DROP SCHEMA uc1 CASCADE;
powershell
# Drop and re-create the local SQLite schema by hand (check_sql.py does this for you)
@'
import sqlite3
con = sqlite3.connect(".data/uc1.db")
for name in ("uc1_drop.sql", "uc1_create.sql", "uc1_seed.sql"):
    con.executescript(open("usecase1/sql/" + name, encoding="utf-8").read())
'@ | uv run --no-project python -

15.2 Reading a golden mismatch

A failing line reads FAIL <case> @ v<version>: missing <what the template found>: expected <what expected.yaml says>. Run that case alone, with the report:

powershell
uv run --no-project --with rdflib==7.6.0 --with pyshacl==0.40.1 --with pyyaml python usecase1/checks/run_golden.py --case G01 --show-report

--show-report prints the pySHACL validation report for each failing version only. Read it like this (the example is the real v2026.1 report of G01, from 12.6):

output
v2026.1 ['R4', 'R5']
Validation Report
Conforms: False
Results (2):
Constraint Violation in SPARQLConstraintComponent (http://www.w3.org/ns/shacl#SPARQLConstraintComponent):
    Severity: sh:Violation
    Source Shape: uc1shape:DocumentRequirement
    Focus Node: app:APP-1001
    Value Node: app:APP-1001
    Source Constraint: [ … the template's SPARQL query, 35 lines, trimmed here … ]
    Message: R4: Profit and loss statement, signed, dated within 90 days of receipt (§4.3.2)
Constraint Violation in SPARQLConstraintComponent (http://www.w3.org/ns/shacl#SPARQLConstraintComponent):
    Severity: sh:Violation
    Source Shape: uc1shape:DocumentRequirement
    Focus Node: app:APP-1001
    Value Node: app:APP-1001
    Source Constraint: [ … trimmed … ]
    Message: R5: Two most recent monthly bank statements for every account (§4.4)
  • Results (2): the number of missing items the template found.
  • Source Shape, the source shape (the shape that produced a SHACL result; uc1shape:DocumentRequirement means a missing document, any other uc1shape: shape a data defect): uc1shape:DocumentRequirement is the completeness template, i.e. a missing document. Data-quality failures do not appear here: they show as the line … data-quality shapes conform failing, with the first 400 characters of that report.
  • Focus Node, the focus node: the application.
  • Message, the result message (the text of one SHACL result; the template sets it to the requirement's label, so it starts with the requirement id and cites the section): the requirement's label, so it names the id (R4) and the section (§4.3.2).
  • Source Constraint: the template's query, the same for every result; skip it.
  1. Decide which of three things changed: the expectation (expected.yaml), the rule (the version file) or the data (Gxx.ttl). git diff on those three files usually answers it.
  2. For an unexpected extra item: walk the template's steps from 12.6 for that requirement. Does the condition hold on the application or a party? Is the document's type, or a narrower one, right? Is ageAtReceiptDays within maxAgeDays? Is it signed when required? Is monthsBeforeReceipt within 1…mostRecentMonths, and are the months distinct?
  3. For a missing item (expected but not found): the same walk, looking for the document that wrongly satisfies it, often a derived value written wrongly (check_consistency recomputes them) or a condition that does not hold.
  4. For a passing case, --show-report prints nothing extra; use the snippet in 12.6 to see the full report.

15.3 FAQ

Why not edit the taxonomy directly in the .ttl file?

Because VocBench is its source of truth. Edits there go through the validation workflow and are kept in the project's history, and the next export would overwrite any hand edit to the file. Editing the file would create a second source of truth that skips approval. The seed was typed once, only to bootstrap the project.

Why are dates written as midnight date-times?

OWL 2 has no xsd:date, and HermiT, also inside Protégé, rejects it. Dates are therefore xsd:dateTime at 00:00:00 in every .ttl file; the SQL keeps real DATE columns, and the bridge writes them at midnight.

Can the LLM decide completeness?

No. Completeness is decided by the deterministic completeness engine: the template and the requirements as data, the same answer every time and testable against the golden cases. The LLM only words the letter (Phase 5), and the verifier rejects any draft whose items differ from the engine's. Classifier suggestions are confirmed by a person in the review queue when unsure.

Where is the source of truth for X?

XSource of truth
Names (IRIs, classes, properties, tables, columns)usecase1/README.md
TaxonomyVocBench project uc1-loss-mitigation, exported to usecase1/taxonomy/uc1-taxonomy.skos.ttl (the seed until step 1.1u)
Ontology modelusecase1/ontology/uc1-core.ttl
Requirements of a guide versionusecase1/ontology/investor-a-<version>.ttl
How completeness is computedusecase1/ontology/uc1-requirements.shapes.ttl
Expected answersusecase1/golden/expected.yaml (compliance)
Tablesusecase1/sql/uc1_create.sql
Holidaysuc1_holiday, seeded from usecase1/sql/uc1_seed.sql
A decision once madeuc1_evaluation, uc1_evaluation_item, uc1_evaluation_item_document (Phase 2)
Guide wording for citationsthe guide documents in RAG-OS (Phase 4)
What Use Case 1 added, and how to remove itusecase1/CHANGES.md

How do I remove Use Case 1?

Follow "Removal, in order" in usecase1/CHANGES.md: delete the guide documents in RAG-OS, revert the Admin hook and menu link, delete .data/uc1.db and drop schema uc1, remove the [usecase1] extra and the uc1 marker, delete tests/usecase1/, usecase1/, docs/UseCase1.html and docs/UseCase1_DetailedImplementation.html (this runbook; CHANGES step 5), delete the VocBench project; then git status must show only docs/use-case-1.md. Phase 6 rehearses exactly this on a scratch copy (section 11).

17Glossary#

Every term that gets a bracketed note in the text is defined here with the same words (one source).

TermWhat it is and why we use it
--native-tlsA uv flag that trusts the Windows certificate store; needed when a proxy or antivirus re-signs HTTPS traffic.
.asc signatureA detached GPG signature published next to a download; verifying it proves the zip is the one the publisher built.
.dockerignoreThe list of paths left out of the Docker build context; it excludes *.md (except README.md), tests and .data.
access tagsA document's department, region and clearance in RAG-OS; a person sees it only if their own attributes match.
acknowledgement noticeThe Reg X written notice due within 5 business days of receipt: it says the application is complete, or lists the documents still missing.
Active ontology tabProtégé's tab that shows the open ontology's IRI, its annotations and the ontologies it imports.
Admin workbenchThe planned 'Use Case 1' page in RAG-OS Admin for reviewing, validating and processing (not editing).
ageAtReceiptDaysDerived: days from a document's date to the date part of the application's received time.
altLabelA synonym of a concept (e.g. 'P&L'); the classifier and lookups use synonyms.
annotation propertyA note-like property ignored by the reasoner; we use two as rule parameters (onProperty, expectedValue).
APIRouterFastAPI's object that groups related endpoints under one URL prefix; one router per feature, mounted by the app factory.
app factorycreate_app() in src/rag_os/api/app.py: the one function that builds the FastAPI app and mounts every router.
append-onlyRows are only ever inserted, never updated or deleted, so every past decision stays exactly as it was made.
applicability conditionWhen a requirement applies: a property (onProperty) that must have a value (expectedValue) on the application or one of its parties.
artifact gateThe Phase 1 exit check: every artifact proven by its tool and checks, and signed off by its owners, before any engine code.
audit logThe rag_os.audit logger RAG-OS uses for security-relevant actions; publish proposals and approvals are written there.
axiom allow-listA check that only permits agreed kinds of statements in the core, so a stray 'functional' or 'equivalent' axiom can't sneak in.
Base URIThe namespace a VocBench project mints IRIs in; ours is https://rag-os.example/uc1/taxonomy, so concept IRIs end #<notation>.
blank nodeAn RDF node without a global IRI (e.g. a document's signature struct); its internal name changes on every run, so comparisons must ignore it.
BLOCKINGA finding that makes the output wrong or unsafe; the step cannot close until it is fixed and re-checked.
bootstrapThe one-time first fill of an empty system (here: loading the seed).
branchA separate line of work in git; Phase 1 work lives on a branch until its pull request is merged.
bridgeThe planned code that turns uc1_* rows into an RDF graph, adding derived values.
broaderThe parent link in a taxonomy hierarchy (Paystub broader Income).
brown fieldA setting where data already exists in other systems, as opposed to starting from nothing.
business dayAny day except Saturday, Sunday or a legal public holiday (Reg X counting).
catalog driftcatalog-v001.xml points at another file than the checks expect, e.g. still the seed after the VocBench export exists.
catalog switchEditing catalog-v001.xml so the taxonomy import points at the VocBench export instead of the seed.
catalog-v001.xmlA lookup file next to the ontology files that tells Protégé (and the checks) which local file holds each imported ontology.
chunkA passage of a document that RAG-OS indexes and retrieves; citations point at chunks.
citationA pointer from an answer to the exact place in a source document (document, section heading, snippet).
classA kind of thing in the ontology, e.g. Application.
classic builderDocker's pre-BuildKit build engine; ACR Tasks (az acr build) only has this, so no RUN --mount, COPY --link or heredocs.
classifierThe RAG-OS component that suggests a document's type from its text; low confidence goes to the review queue.
classifier accuracy setSME-labelled sample documents, one folder per document type, run through the RAG-OS classifier to measure how often it picks the right type.
closed worldSHACL's assumption that the data is complete as given, which is what a completeness check needs.
competency questionA question the model must be able to answer; written first, it keeps the model small and purposeful.
completeness engineBridge + pySHACL + template: decides which requirements are missing for one application.
completeness templateThe single SHACL-SPARQL rule (uc1-requirements.shapes.ttl) that checks every requirement of the governing version.
compose projectA named docker compose stack (-p name) with its own containers and volumes, so a rehearsal cannot touch the shared stack.
concept schemeOne controlled list in the taxonomy, e.g. uc1_doc_type; it becomes one RAG-OS facet.
conftest.pyPytest's per-folder setup file; tests/usecase1/conftest.py skips the folder when the usecase1 extra is not installed.
containerAn isolated Linux process with its own files, run by Docker; here it runs PostgreSQL 16.
contract mapThe copy of the README naming contract hard-coded in a check script (CONTRACT, HIERARCHY, EXPECTED_…); changed in the same pull request as the README.
converterThe planned code that turns the VocBench SKOS export into the facets YAML the RAG-OS classifier reads.
correlation idAn id attached to one request and every log line it causes, so one action can be traced end to end.
cross-artifact consistencycheck_consistency.py: the taxonomy, ontology, shapes, golden graphs and SQL seed describe one model.
data-quality shapeA SHACL rule about the data itself (e.g. every application has a received date); its failures are fixed by the servicer, never sent to a borrower.
datatype propertyA value on a thing, e.g. receivedAt (a date-time).
date at midnightDates are written as xsd:dateTime at 00:00:00 because OWL 2 has no xsd:date and HermiT rejects it.
DCT (Dublin Core terms)A W3C-recognised vocabulary for titles and descriptions of files and vocabularies.
definitionA one-sentence meaning of a concept; it becomes the facet value's description for the classifier.
Definition of DoneThe conditions every step must meet before it can be closed.
derived valueA value computed from stored facts when needed (document age, governing version), never stored itself.
dev tokenA locally signed JWT for a demo principal (POST /api/dev/token), available only when DEV_AUTH_ENABLED=true; used by API tests.
disjoint classesClasses that can share no member; lets the reasoner catch a borrower typed as a document.
docker composeDocker's tool that starts the services described in the repo's docker-compose.yml; Phase 1 starts only its postgres service.
Docker DesktopRuns containers on Windows; we use it only to start a local PostgreSQL for the portability proof.
due dateThe 5th business day after receipt; the deadline for the acknowledgement notice.
effective datesA guide version's effectiveFromDate and effectiveToDate, both inclusive; no end date means open-ended.
Entities tabProtégé's tab with sub-tabs for classes, object properties, data properties, annotation properties and individuals.
esbuildThe bundler that turns chat-ui/src/*.ts into the static files nginx serves (npm run build).
evaluation logThe append-only rows in uc1_evaluation, uc1_evaluation_item and uc1_evaluation_item_document that freeze one decision.
expected.yamlThe file listing each golden case's expected missing requirement ids per guide version.
Export DataVocBench's export function (Global Data Management → Export Data): writes the project to a file, here Turtle.
facetA RAG-OS tag dimension (e.g. doc_type); each value can be filtered and classified.
FacetDefRAG-OS's Pydantic model of one facet in facets.yaml (name, field, label, hierarchical, classify, values); the converter's output must validate against it.
facets versionThe version: number at the top of a RAG-OS facets file; bump it so the classifier refreshes its cached prototypes.
FacetSchemaThe RAG-OS model of a whole facets file: an integer version plus the list of facets; the classifier caches its prototypes per version.
feature flagA setting (here UC1_ENABLED) that switches a feature on or off without a code change; off by default.
findingOne problem a reviewer records: severity, location, evidence, the fix asked for and what happened to it.
focus nodeThe thing a SHACL shape checks; here every uc1:Application.
four-eyes publishA change goes live only after a second, different person approves it.
front matterA YAML block at the top of a Markdown file (between --- lines); RAG-OS reads effective_date from it.
frozen snapshotThe evaluation row plus its item rows and linked documents, written once: the version used, every outcome and the due date.
functional propertyA property that may have at most one value; lets the reasoner catch e.g. an item that is both Met and Missing.
gateThe agreed exit check of a step or phase; work moves on only when every gate item holds.
gitVersion control; every artifact is a file in git, so every change is reviewed as a diff.
git diffShows line by line what changed in a file since the last commit; the reviewer's main evidence.
golden caseA hand-written test application with the answer fixed in advance by the compliance view.
golden runEvaluating every golden case under every guide version and comparing with the expected missing items (run_golden.py).
governing versionThe guide version in effect on the application's received date; the one its completeness is judged against.
group conceptA taxonomy concept that only groups others (Application, Income, Assets, Hardship, Tax, Property); never a classification target.
guideline versionOne published version of an investor's servicing guide, with effective dates.
heading pathThe chain of Markdown headings above a chunk, joined with ' > ' (e.g. 'Investor A … > 4.3.2 Self-employed income'); a citation's heading field.
healthyDocker's status for a service whose health check passes; for postgres the check is pg_isready.
HermiTA reasoner bundled with Protégé: software that checks the ontology's logic for contradictions; we use it to prove the model is consistent.
HistoryA VocBench project option that records every change with who and when; it can only be switched on when the project is created.
imageThe container built from the Dockerfile; it runs the API, worker and jobs, and holds only what the Dockerfile copies in.
Implicitly validate loaded dataA Load Data option that accepts the imported content as already approved; right for a one-time bootstrap, wrong for later edits.
import closureAn ontology plus everything it imports, directly or indirectly; tools load and check them together.
inconsistent ontologyAn ontology whose statements contradict each other; nothing inferred from it can be trusted until it is fixed.
independent reviewA fresh reviewer (person or sub-agent) checks the output against the design and lists findings; they do not edit.
individualOne concrete thing in the ontology, e.g. requirement ia26:R4.
interfaceA shared shape several classes implement, e.g. «Evidence» for every submitted document.
invariant queryA SQL query that must return no rows on valid data (e.g. two primary borrowers on one application); any row it returns is a violation.
inverse pairTwo names for one link read from each end, e.g. documents / application.
IRIA web-style global identifier for a thing, e.g. https://rag-os.example/uc1/taxonomy#Paystub; like a primary key that is unique worldwide.
isomorphic graphsTwo RDF graphs that are identical once blank nodes are renamed consistently; rdflib.compare.isomorphic tests exactly this.
JAVA_HOMEThe environment variable naming the Java installation; check_ontology.py runs HermiT with its bin\java.exe.
JSON output modeCalling the LLM port with json_output=True so the reply is a JSON object the code can parse and verify.
lazy importAn import placed inside the code path that needs it, so the module and its libraries load only when the flag is on.
ledger (CHANGES.md)The list of every Use Case 1 artifact and how to remove it, so the use case stays removable.
legal public holidayA US federal holiday as observed; it is not a business day for the Reg X clock and is kept in uc1_holiday.
lexicalization modelHow a VocBench project stores labels: plain SKOS literals (ours) or SKOS-XL label resources.
lineageRecorded origin of each row (source system, key, extraction time), so any fact can be traced back.
LLM portRAG-OS's single interface for calling a language model (LlmProvider.complete), with token accounting.
Load DataVocBench's import function (Global Data Management → Load Data): reads an RDF file into the project.
load the seedCopy the seed file's concepts into the empty VocBench project, once, through Global Data Management → Load Data.
maxAgeDaysThe oldest a satisfying document may be at receipt, in days.
minCountHow many satisfying documents, or distinct months, a requirement needs (default 1).
monthsBeforeReceiptDerived: calendar months from the month a document's period ends to the month of receipt.
mostRecentMonthsA window of whole months before the month of receipt (1 = the month before) in which statement periods must end.
mutation testChanging one fact on purpose and checking the result changes as it should; proves a test is not passing by accident.
n-ary relationA relationship among more than two things, modelled as its own object (a Requirement links version, document type, condition, recency and section).
named volumeDocker storage that survives container restarts; where the database files live.
naming contractusecase1/README.md: every prefix, concept id, class, property, table and column name; a name changes there first, then everywhere.
NITA cosmetic point (wording, order, spelling); fixed if cheap, never blocks a step.
notationA concept's short code (e.g. ProfitAndLoss); here it equals the IRI's local name and the RAG-OS facet value id.
object propertyA link between two things, e.g. documents (Application → SubmittedDocument).
OData filterThe filter language of the search index (e.g. effective_date eq '2026-01-01'); RAG-OS builds access and facet clauses in it.
ON_ERROR_STOPA psql variable: stop at the first failing statement (exit code 3) instead of carrying on and hiding the error.
Only meThe upload option that makes a document readable by the uploader alone; wrong for guides, which the servicing team must read.
ontologyThe formal model of the domain's things and relationships, precise enough for software to check.
ontology IRIThe identifier in a file's owl:Ontology header; the catalog and Protégé find each file by it.
open worldOWL's assumption that anything not stated may still be true, so OWL alone can't say a document is missing.
OpenAPIThe API description FastAPI generates at /api/openapi.json and shows at /api/docs; a new router appears there with its tag.
optional extraA named group of extra dependencies in pyproject.toml [project.optional-dependencies]; installed only on request, e.g. --extra usecase1.
OWLThe W3C ontology language: classes, properties and their rules.
OWL 2 DLThe decidable profile of OWL that reasoners like HermiT accept; our files must stay inside it.
owl:importsA statement that one ontology includes another; investor-a-2026.1.ttl imports the core, which imports the taxonomy.
owl:versionInfoA version annotation in an ontology header; each requirements file carries its guide version label there.
owlready2A Python library that can run HermiT from a script; our checks use it for an automatic consistency test.
PlaywrightBrowser automation used to test web pages (links, rendering, phone width).
portability proofRunning the same SQL scripts unchanged on a second database engine (PostgreSQL 16) to prove they do not depend on SQLite quirks.
positive controlA valid example that must be accepted; proves a check rejects bad data for the right reason, not because it rejects everything.
PostgreSQLThe production-grade database RAG-OS uses in Azure; we prove our SQL also runs there, in a separate uc1 schema.
precisionOf the items the engine flags as missing, the share that really were missing; a wrong item costs the borrower time, so it is weighted hardest.
prefixA short alias for a namespace, e.g. uc1tx: = https://rag-os.example/uc1/taxonomy#, so IRIs stay readable.
prefLabelA concept's one preferred name per language.
principalThe signed-in caller as RAG-OS sees it: subject id, attributes and roles.
problem+jsonThe error format RAG-OS returns for every failure (status, title, detail, correlation id); domain errors map to it automatically.
prompt injectionText inside the model's input (e.g. a guide snippet) that tries to change its instructions; the verifier must catch its effects.
property assertionA stated value of an object or data property on one individual, e.g. R4-recency maxAgeDays 90.
property pathA SPARQL pattern that follows links, e.g. skos:broader* = the concept itself or any of its ancestors.
property testA test that tries many generated inputs to check a rule always holds (e.g. due date is always a business day).
proposalA VocBench edit that waits for a validator; it is not part of the export until it is approved.
prototypeThe embedding of a facet value's 'label: description. Also known as: synonyms' text; the classifier picks the value whose prototype is closest to the document.
ProtégéA desktop editor for ontologies (OWL) from Stanford; the knowledge engineer edits the model and the requirements here.
PROV-OThe W3C provenance vocabulary: records which guide version an evaluation used and what it generated.
psqlPostgreSQL's command-line client; used inside the container to run the SQL scripts.
public-configThe unauthenticated GET /api/public-config endpoint that hands the browser non-secret settings before sign-in; Admin reads feature flags from it.
pull requestA proposed change in git that reviewers approve before it is merged.
PydanticThe Python library RAG-OS uses for settings and data models with validation.
pySHACLA Python library that checks a data graph against SHACL rules and reports every violation; it is the completeness engine's rule runner.
pytest markerA label on tests (here uc1) registered in pyproject.toml; lets a team select or skip one group with -m.
RACIPer activity: who is Responsible (does it), Accountable (approves it), Consulted (asked first) and Informed (told afterwards).
RDFThe W3C data model of triples that the taxonomy, ontology and golden cases are written in.
RDF storeA database for triples; Semantic Turkey keeps each VocBench project in its own store, separate from any file.
rdflibA Python library to read, build, query and write RDF graphs; used by the checks and, later, the bridge.
rdfs:seeAlsoA link to where more information lives; each GuideSection points at its section of the guide.
reasonerSoftware that works out what logically follows from an ontology and detects contradictions (e.g. HermiT).
recallOf the items that really were missing, the share the engine flagged.
recency ruleHow recent, signed or how many documents a requirement needs (maxAgeDays, signatureRequired, minCount, mostRecentMonths).
record_tokensRAG-OS telemetry function that counts LLM tokens per provider, model and purpose; callers invoke it, providers do not.
relevance barThe minimum search score RAG-OS needs before it treats a passage as an answer source.
removal rehearsalFollowing the ledger's removal steps on a scratch copy to prove Use Case 1 leaves no trace.
Render by labelA Protégé view option that shows readable labels (e.g. 'R4: Profit and loss…') instead of IRIs.
require_roleThe RAG-OS FastAPI dependency (src/rag_os/api/deps.py) that answers 403 unless the caller holds one of the named roles.
requirementOne rule of a guide version: which document, when it applies, how recent, which section.
requirement idThe stable short id of a requirement within a guide version (R1 to R8); engine, evaluation log, letter and verifier all speak in these ids.
requirements fileOne Turtle file per guide version (investor-a-<version>.ttl) holding that version's requirements as data.
result messageThe text of one SHACL result; the template sets it to the requirement's label, so it starts with the requirement id and cites the section.
review queueThe RAG-OS Admin list of documents whose automatic tags need a person to confirm.
ROBOTA command-line ontology tool; we used it once to confirm the files are valid OWL 2 DL.
round tripLoad the seed into VocBench, export it again and prove nothing was lost or altered (check_taxonomy.py --roundtrip).
ruffThe Python linter and import sorter configured in pyproject.toml [tool.ruff]; the repo's code-style gate.
run_all.pyThe one command that runs every Phase 1 check and prints PASS/FAIL per area.
scaffoldThe empty structure a piece of work starts from: folders, the naming contract, the ledger and the check runner.
scratch cloneA throw-away copy of the repository (git clone or git worktree) where destructive steps can be rehearsed safely.
search_pathPostgreSQL's list of schemas used for unqualified table names; SET search_path TO uc1 makes the scripts create and read uc1.uc1_* tables.
seedA starter file holding the agreed first version of the taxonomy; used once to fill an empty VocBench project.
semantic modelThe relational tables (uc1_*) that hold the facts of each case in business terms.
Semantic TurkeyThe server VocBench runs on; it stores each project's data and is started by bin\semanticturkey.bat.
SHACLThe W3C language for rules a data graph must satisfy; the validation report lists each violation.
SHACL Advanced FeaturesAn optional SHACL extension (rules, custom targets); pySHACL enables it with -a; we do not need it.
SHACL CoreThe basic part of SHACL (cardinality, datatype, value checks); our data-quality shapes use only this.
SHACL-SPARQLA SHACL constraint written as a SPARQL query; every row the query returns is one violation.
shadow runRunning the engine on past applications next to the people who handled them, to measure precision before switching over.
shapeOne SHACL rule set with a target, e.g. 'every Application must satisfy its requirements'.
SHOULD-FIXA real weakness that does not break the output today; fixed in the same step unless the owner records why not.
sign-offA named owner's recorded approval (in the pull request or the review record) that an artifact says what they mean.
signatureRequiredA recency parameter: every satisfying document must be signed.
SKOSThe W3C vocabulary for taxonomies: concepts, schemes, preferred/alternative labels, broader/narrower.
SKOS conceptOne entry of the taxonomy, e.g. Paystub.
SKOS-XLA SKOS extension where every label is a resource of its own; we use plain SKOS labels because the checks and the converter read plain literals.
source shapeThe shape that produced a SHACL result; uc1shape:DocumentRequirement means a missing document, any other uc1shape: shape a data defect.
SPARQLThe query language for RDF; our SHACL template is written as a SPARQL query.
specified IRIA VocBench concept created with an IRI you type (local name = notation) instead of a generated random c_… IRI.
SQLAlchemy CoreThe SQL toolkit RAG-OS already depends on; text() queries with :named parameters run unchanged on SQLite and PostgreSQL.
SQLiteA single-file database built into Python; the local home of the uc1_* tables (.data/uc1.db).
Start reasonerProtégé's Reasoner menu command that runs the selected reasoner (HermiT) over the open ontology and its imports.
static lintA check that reads a file's text for forbidden patterns without running it.
stored directionThe one side of an inverse pair actually written in data and read by the rules; the other side is inferred only.
structA group of related fields kept together as one value, e.g. signature = signed + date.
taxonomyThe controlled words of the domain, arranged in a hierarchy with synonyms.
taxonomy.yamlThe planned facets file the converter generates from the VocBench export, in the format the RAG-OS classifier reads.
TBoxThe schema part of an ontology (classes, properties, axioms), kept apart from the data (the ABox).
Temurin JDK 21A free Java runtime from Eclipse Adoptium; VocBench needs Java 17 or later.
test-firstChange the expected answer before the rule, watch the check fail, then make it pass; proves the check sees the change.
token budgetA cap on the LLM tokens a run may spend; checked before each call and decremented after, as the RAG-OS hybrid classifier does.
top conceptA concept at the top of a scheme's hierarchy (skos:topConceptOf), e.g. Income evidence.
tripleOne RDF statement: subject – predicate – object, e.g. APP-1001 receivedAt 2026-02-06.
Turtle (.ttl)A readable text format for RDF; every ontology, taxonomy and golden file here is Turtle.
uc1 schemaA separate PostgreSQL namespace holding only Use Case 1 tables; removable with DROP SCHEMA uc1 CASCADE.
UC1_ENABLEDThe planned setting that switches the Use Case 1 Admin workbench on; off means nothing of Use Case 1 shows.
unsatisfiable classA class that cannot have any member without a contradiction; reasoners list it under owl:Nothing, usually in red.
UTF-8The text encoding of every file here; the Windows console defaults to cp1252, which cannot print §, → or ✓.
uvThe Python package and environment manager used in this repo; `uv run --with …` fetches tools into a throw-away environment.
uv lockThe command that re-resolves dependencies and rewrites uv.lock; required after any dependency change in pyproject.toml, and the lock file is committed with it.
validation reportSHACL's output: conforms true/false plus one result per violation (focus node, message, source shape).
validation workflowVocBench's propose → approve process: an edit stays pending until a validator accepts it.
value partitionA fixed set of allowed values modelled as individuals, e.g. Met / Missing / NotApplicable.
verifierA deterministic check that the drafted letter lists exactly the engine's missing items, nothing added or dropped.
version diffThe per-requirement comparison of two guide versions (changed, added, removed); check_ontology.py asserts the expected one.
version IRIAn owl:versionIRI naming one specific version of an ontology, so tools can tell versions apart.
VocBench 3A web-based editor for taxonomies (SKOS); subject-matter experts maintain the controlled words here, with review before publishing.
VS CodeThe code editor used for the text artifacts (SQL, SHACL, YAML, Turtle).
what-if evaluationEvaluating an application against a guide version that does not govern it (--version), for comparison only; never recorded.
wingetWindows' built-in package manager; one command installs Temurin, uv or Docker Desktop.
WSL 2Windows Subsystem for Linux; Docker Desktop runs its Linux engine on it.
